--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed from the graph to completely disconnect the two black nodes (H and Q), we need to identify all paths between these nodes and find the smallest set of edges whose removal will eliminate all such paths.

### Steps to Solve:

1. **Identify the black nodes:** The black nodes are H and Q.
2. **Trace all paths from H to Q:** We need to follow all possible paths between H and Q, noting intermediate nodes and edges.
3. **Find the critical edges:** Determine the smallest set of edges whose removal will disconnect all paths between H and Q.

### Paths from H to Q:

1. **Path 1:** H → G → B → E → F → J → I → Q
2. **Path 2:** H → G → B → E → F → J → Q
3. **Path 3:** H → G → B → F → J → I → Q
4. **Path 4:** H → G → B → F → J → Q

### Critical Edges:

To disconnect H and Q, we need to identify the minimal set of edges whose removal will eliminate all paths between H and Q. The critical edges are:

- (G, B): Removal of this edge will disconnect many paths involving G and B.
- (F, J): Removal of this edge will disconnect paths involving F and J.

Removing these two edges will ensure that there are no remaining paths between H and Q.

### Answer:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(G, B), (F, J)]

To analyze the graph to determine the shortest path between the two black nodes, P and R, we need to trace the connections between these nodes through the intermediary nodes.

1. Start at node P. P connects to nodes B, E, S, F, Q, I, and T.
2. From each of these nodes, we need to identify any paths that lead to node R.

- From B: no direct path to R
- From E: connects to T and C => C connects to G => G connects to J => J connects to R: Path: [P, E, T, C, G, J, R] (5 edges)
- From S: connects to N, O => No paths from N or O to R
- From F: connects to E, T => E, and T have been checked already.
- From Q: connects to I, J, H => H connects to G => G connects to J => J connects to R: Path: [P, Q, H, G, J, R] (4 edges)
- From T: connects to E, C, B => E and B have been checked already. C connects to G => G connects to J => J connects to R: Path: [P, T, C, G, J, R] (4 edges)
- From I: connects to Q, J => Q and J have been checked already.

Now let's determine the shortest among valid paths:
- [P, E, T, C, G, J, R] (5 edges)
- [P, Q, H, G, J, R] (4 edges)
- [P, T, C, G, J, R] (4 edges)

The shortest path from P to R is thus through either [P, Q, H, G, J, R] or [P, T, C, G, J, R].

Answer: Length: 4, Path: [P, Q, H, G, J, R] or [P, T, C, G, J, R]

1. **Identify paths from R to P:**
   - R -> C -> G -> J -> H -> I -> Q -> P
   - R -> O -> B -> E -> F -> T -> B -> C -> N -> Q -> P
   - R -> O -> B -> S -> E -> F -> T -> B -> C -> N -> Q -> P
   - R -> O -> B -> F -> T -> B -> C -> N -> Q -> P

2. **Find the minimum cut set:**
   - To disconnect R and P, we need to remove edges that are part of all these paths.
   - Removing the edge (C, N) will break the path through C -> N -> Q -> P.
   - Removing the edge (B, C) will break the path through B -> C -> N -> Q -> P.
   - Removing the edge (Q, P) will directly disconnect Q from P.

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3.
